## 1. CYCLE 2 ENDED -- MAX DAILY LOSS, 23 SEP 13:51Z

Record: `docs/FULL_TRIAL_RECORD_1514582088.md` (`ed1756f`). -$505.34 against
-$500 on the FTMO day; overall -$314.38. The day OPENED about -$330 below
its anchor: 84% of the loss was inventory carried in from earlier days.
The two USD majors lost $320; the six crosses together made +$6. OPT and ALT
held near-identical stacks, so every move was paid twice. Close-out done:
EAs detached, variables cleaned (154 deleted, none left), deal dump kept in
`data/local/deals_dump_20260923_1741.csv` (not committed; reconciles to
MetriX to the cent).

The 80% breaker could not have saved it (it trips on a bad day, not a bad
START); that became C28 and then ADR-160.

## 3. ADR-160 -- ALL-DAY FLOATING-LOSS GATE (EA DONE, PIPSHED D5 PENDING)

No new entries while floating loss (balance - equity) >= 50% of the daily
allowance; clears at 40%. Cancels nothing; exits, carry and ejection run.
Cycle-2 counterfactual: ~$102 better, and 23 Sep survives at ~-$425.
Merged `c22e9ff`. Gated seconds counted by the MAE reporter-lease holder
into `GRIND_SNAPSHOT_GATED_S_<day>`, reported as `gated_seconds` in the
snapshot (stored in pipshed's `detail` JSON today; the column is D5).

Decided and rejected on the way (see TRAPS for the detail):

  resembles what FTMO polices.
- Changing Fleet B's geometry (wider USD adds; a non-USD ring with three

  comparison; the ring/plane design is recorded as a cycle-4 precursor

## 11. UPDATE ~04:00Z 25 SEP -- ADR-162 B1 BUILT; LIVE CARRY-PASS RACE FOUND

- ADR-162 Phase B1 built and tested on branch `adr162-phase-b1` (ADR-162
  s13): tip `3188f72` 2002/2002 on GBPUSD and EURUSD; DeepSeek `73b1a8d`
  checked. NOT merged: the post-audit patch (C54) first. `main` holds
  only this docs commit on top of `8f72d00`; EA code == `9466b22`.
- Operator: no layer is rolled twice; roll on demand deferred (C53);
  ORDER: carry fix (C52), then the lattice (C54 merge, B2 C55, pipshed
  C56). Fleet C (C51) WAITS until those are done.
- DeepSeek T-4 exposed a PRE-EXISTING LIVE defect in the nightly carry
  pass (C52): a layer the queue releases after `Grind_CarryExitPassBegin`
  captured its zero exit ticket gets its accrual committed while its new
  exit stays at yesterday's accrual. I6 fails by one night's swap,
  quarantine cannot repair it, the instance halts after 3 checks, and a
  restart likely fails reconstruction. Any scalp during the ~10-minute
  pass (2 items per 60 s) can trigger it. Cycle 3 and Fleet B run carry ON.
- Evidence, VPS Experts logs 24-25 Sep: 112 fault lines, ALL I3
  transients released after 1 check (C40); the last before the window at
  20:42Z, none in 20:50-21:00Z (window assumes the broker runs GMT+3,
  unverified; the pass writes to the archive, not the log). Latent, not
  fired. Fleet B not checked (same code).
- IF IT FIRES: red banner, INVARIANT_FAIL with I6_LONG_EXIT or
  I6_SHORT_EXIT near the carry window; the instance halts, its resting
  exits stay live. Do NOT restart it; bring it to the chat.
- This chat also learned: PowerShell needs `'HEAD^{tree}'` quoted; a log
  grep for `halted` matches every heartbeat (traps 2026-09-25 B1).

"one of the central planks of this approach. it leans into the fact that
we enter positions randomly and hence should not feel any loyalty to
them, especially if they are wildly underwater." The other plank: "using
the trade data we accrue - which we do excessively in cycle 4" (ROADMAP
s6).

the VPS (cycle 3 is the frozen control; Fleet C is the window fleet, not
built). Numbers: `s4_scalps.py --days 2` after 22:00Z Fri (days 24, 25);

recommendation, not confirmed by the operator). **Reattach with the

open) or before 03:00 ET Monday. Not while closed: `OnInit` can place

## 56. MONDAY 5 OCT ~01:00Z TO ~03:25Z -- THE EUR GRIND; IS THE GRID PLAUSIBLE; MARKOUT AND VARIANCE STUDY

- **Docs s55 pushed** (`3164070`, tree `7e1ce40c`).
- **The EUR grind (status 02:52Z):** open MTM A -$126, B -$329, C -$336,
  D -$241; EURUSD long -$212 and EURGBP long -$150 across B + C + D
  (EURUSD -68 pips since 22:10Z); each IC fleet about NZD +21, AUD -20,
  EUR +14, USD -10 layers. Stuck (every layer rolled, market past the
  next level): EURGBP long B, EURUSD long B and C, NZDCHF long B, C, D.
  No action: re-roll at the Monday build; ejects would cut the anchor's
  window-2 data and not reduce exposure. FTMO (real limit, breaker on) is
  the account to watch: 8/8 long EURUSD and EURGBP, about a quarter of
  its limit on MTM.
- **Operator:** "i am wondering if the whole vision of how to trade fx is
  plausible ... with spot fx you have no such thing to lean against";
  "my belief is that convexity is overpriced ... delta hedging an option
  feels like it is doing what we are doing - is that fair?" Claude: fair,
  with the sign flipped: the grid trades a long-option holder's hedge
  without the option, so it is synthetically SHORT convexity (scalps =
  theta, inventory = gamma loss) with no premium paid; on a random walk
  its expectation is zero before costs; its edge must be mean reversion
  at the scalp scale beyond the inventory scale, and passive fills not
  too adversely selected. Operator: draft the study and start now.
- **`docs/research/markout-variance-study.md` + `research/markout/`
  (first pass, exploratory):** on FTMO minute bars (21 pairs, 20 Sep - 3
  Oct) and 4,518 entry fills: a buy limit PAYS half the spread at the
  fill (the ask triggers it); markouts -1.0 pip at 15 min (adverse
  selection about half a pip beyond the spread), +0.5 at 4 h; **L0 is the
  worst entry (-2.3 at 1 h, -5.0 at 4 h), depth 6-7 the best (+2.2 at
  4 h, +5.4 at 1 d)**; per pair, VR(60) ranks our fills' 1-hour markout
  (Spearman -0.83, nine pairs): GBPUSD, EURGBP, NZDCAD revert; AUDCHF,
  NZDCHF, AUDCAD trended at the hour. Random-walk calibration in
  `cal.py`. Caveats: twelve days, one regime, fills not independent.
  Next: the 1-7 Oct holdout unchanged; IC's own mid; then any C103 / pair
  question to Gemini.

  realised = e*S - (N*a - e)*R = e*R*(S/R - k*), k* = (N*a - e + c)/(e -
  c); under a random walk S/R = k* exactly for every geometry (reflected
  depth uniform on 0..N; optional stopping), so the edge is reversion
  within D = N*a and a low k* is not better (cheaper rolls come more
  often; costs bite tight geometries twice). B 5 Oct 22:00Z - 7 Oct

- **The research notes (~22:40Z-22:55Z): `docs/research/grid-as-variance-trade.md`.**
  The clean two-sided lattice (e = a, no cap) has an EXACT P&L:
  (a * n)/2 - dP^2/(2a): half the path travelled at step a minus the
  squared net move over 2a (checked by simulation to the pip). Zero mean
  under a random walk but a lopsided distribution (median about +0.27 x
  path, rare large losses: short volatility in the displacement). The cap
  turns the quadratic cost linear beyond D = N*a (a Huber shape; rolling
  drags the corridor): a CORRIDOR VARIANCE TRADE, long variance at the
  fine scale e, short at the coarse scale D. **Three dials -> two scales:
  e and D (k* + 1 = D/e), sizing by a = D/N.** Edge ~ the fall of V(s) =
  s^2 x (zig-zag legs of s per day) between e and D (flat = random walk).
  First look, FTMO 5-7 Oct minute data: flat from 2 to 10 pips on every
  pair; EURUSD rising 10 -> 28 (the EUR sell-off); 0-5 legs at D (weeks
  needed). Literature map: move-based hedging (Fukasawa; Rosenbaum-Tankov
  2014), the Whalley-Wilmott cube-root band (Zakamouline), GLFT inventory
  market making, Bertram / Leung-Li OU round trips, Lo-MacKinlay variance
  ratios, Taranto-Khan grids and ruin. Conjectures C-1..C-4 (S/R / k*
  tracks V(e)/V(D); shape persists; compass results follow the slope; two
  scales explain the geometry results). Operator ~22:48Z: "the excellent
  research"; the handover point is now (this chat was compacted once;
  pipshed C137 needs a fresh read of `app.py` and its tests).

## 67. THURSDAY 8 OCT ~00:40Z TO ~01:40Z -- THE LITERATURE READ; THE OPERATOR'S TWO CORRECTIONS; THE EDGE RATIO rho; CALIBRATE A REPLAY ON EURUSD; HANDOVER

- **s66b pushed** (`5a5400a`, tree `c7779dc9`).
- **The literature (~00:45-01:00Z), three readers in parallel** (GLFT
  inventory market making; the FX scaling laws and the Alpha Engine; OU
  round trips, hedging bands, grid papers): written up in
  `docs/research/grid-as-variance-trade.md` s9 with each source marked
  read / not read. Headlines: geometry alone cannot earn (every source);
  FX directional-change exponents put USD majors trendy and EUR / GBP /
  CHF crosses reverting between 5 and 70 pips (2002-2007 data); the
  overshoot law is random-walk behaviour, our roll risk is the tail beyond
  exp(-x/e); a* ~ 2c / (1 - VR) for an unanchored lattice (a reader's
  derivation); GLFT's add is linear in sigma only while the cap is loose
  (with a tight cap the cap sets a widening spacing: a reader's check);
  hedging on price moves is the efficient scheme.
- **Operator ~01:00Z, two corrections** ("ours is not a pure strategy
  where we mark down entry levels at the start"): (1) rolls follow the
  market to the ranges where it clusters (lattice-local, not anchored; the
  M1 vs M5 point: the ratio against sqrt(5) is the information); (2)
  inventory drives entry: the empty side's L0 floats at W (a continuation
  leg at the small scale beside the stack's reversion bet). So three
  scales, W, e, D (s10). Operator ~01:07Z: **no time-series analysis or
  signals**; extract what we can from our own trade history (the
  forward-spread range analogy); the M1 / M5 study parked.
- **The study export** (operator, ~01:10Z): `study_2026-10-08_0110.jsonl`
  in Downloads (`--export-study --days 16`; 21,999 fill rows, 5,195
  scalps, 4,418 events, 1,393 config events from 22 Sep).
- **The edge ratio rho = (S + R) e / (R D)** (s11): 1 on a random walk at
  any geometry; hurdle e / (e - c) (~1.08-1.16); open losses count as
  rolls in waiting. First look on D (flat from 1 Oct; reconciles to its
  balance after commission): GBPUSD 1.98, NZDCAD 7.5 (thin), AUDNZD 1.33,
  NZDCHF 1.02, AUDCHF 0.81, CADCHF 0.84, EURGBP 0.79, AUDCAD 0.77,
  EURUSD 0.74. Realised-only flatters; per-day filters bias low; noise
  ~1/sqrt(R). C141.
- **Operator ~01:19Z: AUDCHF from 0.81 to above 1 by a compass probe?**
  Only by finding scales where it reverts; a two-day round cannot resolve
  it (~35-45% noise); a replay sweep first.
- **Operator ~01:22Z-01:31Z: "we were loathe to do a backtest because we
  felt it did not replicate real life ... pick a fxpair with a lot of
  trade points we can calibrate against and see if we can replicate the
  trade history we observed"; "getting a reliable replay would be a huge

  - the next add is `anchor -/+ add` (`Grind_ComputeAddTarget`), already per
    side;
  - `Grind_EnsureAddNext` re-quotes a resting add every tick when its target
    has moved more than the deadband, so a target that follows the gap needs
    NO new send path;
  - a side at the cap can never be the light side (the cap blocks adds), so
    the lattice and the roll always see the configured add;
  - no add value is read in reconciliation, the exit queue or the carry
    pass;
  - all 34 live presets: fill-time place on, entry horizon off, deadband 2,
    gate 0.
- **The proposal:** one input `InpLightAddByGap` (empty = off), a list of
  FRACTIONS of the side's own add by gap (the last entry holds), NO floor in
  pips; a live target through the existing re-quote;
  FATAL at init on a bad table; the heartbeat carries the effective add. v1
  leaves out C103 (the empty side's L0), C146, C127, heavy-side widening and
  C147 (1).
- **Found while writing:** a step table re-quotes at its threshold on every
  heavy-side scalp there; a faster light side means the heavy side is gated
  longer and 8/8 comes more often; the fleet guard (153-159 of 194 tonight)
  will not hold nine books near 8/8.

### s84 continued, 5 (Fri 16 Oct ~13:50Z to ~16:55Z): WHY THE RANGE BLEEDS: THE ROLLED EXIT, MEASURED

- `main` `e732eed` pushed (s84e; tree `548f8ebb`).
- **"no roll" on B and C EURGBP (operator's screenshot ~13:51Z):** pipshed's label for a capped side with
  every layer rolled; written before ADR-165, so with re-roll on it misleads (the side keeps re-rolling).
  Backlog C156.
- **The operator (~16:32Z): why "loss after loss" in a market that went down then up ("one might even say
  it is still range trading").** Claude: a rolled layer's exit trails the market one exit behind the
  extreme (ADR-162 / ADR-165), so the loss is realised on the first one-exit retracement: a trailing stop
  that fires at every turn of a range wider than the stack (7 x add). In a V wider than the stack, rolling
  is strictly worse than holding; in a one-way move they cost about the same.
- **Measured (operator's exports of B's GBPUSD and EURGBP archives, 16:40Z / 16:50Z; `research/roll_recovery/`,
  script + tests (a mutation keeping OUT_BY fails two) + `results_2026-10-16.md` + `inputs.sha256`):**
  GBPUSD (stack 63 pips) 26 rolled closes, -1,970 pips, 18 later back through their original entry
  (median 18.4 h), none older than 48 h unrecovered; EURGBP (stack 21 pips) 45, -1,046 pips, 24 back (median
  20.8 h), 18 never (early-October trend longs). Quiet days steady, roll days wipe them out. Claude's first
  draft counted OUT_BY prices as market prices (every close "recovered"): caught and fixed before use.
- **The lever:** the ROLLED-EXIT distance (k x exit, or the original target): nothing changes in quiet
  weeks, which answers the operator's objection to wider adds ("there is no reason to assume next week the
  ranges dont revert to 40 pips"). Ranked first for the autopsy. Its cost: the stack stays full through the
  turn.
- **The operator: "perhaps we need ALT back?"** Claude: two arms diversify the regime bet, not the
  inventory (both load in a trend; 0.01 minimum lots); a cheaper form is one geometry per IC fleet (close
  to the compass rounds stopped 13 Oct: the operator's call). After the trial.
- All of it in `docs/research/roll-geometry-notes-2026-10-15.md` s7-s8 (operator: "lets document now - i think we end up forgetting stuff").
- No fleet action since A's breaker (s84 continued 4).
